Aidan has a lot of sugar cubes which he finds he can build into 
one larger cube or spread out to make a square one layer high.

What is the smallest number of sugar cubes that Aidan could have?

<div class="question subquestion" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

The diagram shows a pyramid made up of $30$ cubes, each of which 
measures $1 \ \text{cm}$ by $1 \ \text{cm}$ by $1 \ \text{cm}$.

![papers/bancrofts-school/2016/sample-paper-2/s02-q06-s02-i00.png](/assets/papers/bancrofts-school/2016/sample-paper-2/s02-q06-s02-i00.png "papers/bancrofts-school/2016/sample-paper-2/s02-q06-s02-i00.png")

tareaperimeter

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

Four $1\ \text{cm}$ squares fit inside a $2\ \text{cm}$ square as shown here.

|     |     |
|:---:|:---:|
|     |     |

How many $1\ \text{cm}$ equilateral triangles would fit inside a $3\ \text{cm}$ equilateral triangle?

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">Four $1\ \text{cm}$ squares fit inside a $2\ \text{cm}$ square as shown here.
<emptyline>
|     |     |
|:---:|:---:|
|     |     |
<emptyline>
How many $1\ \text{cm}$ equilateral triangles would fit inside a $3\ \text{cm}$ equilateral triangle?
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

In the square:

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Side of big square} &= 2 \ \text{cm} \\
\text{Side of small square} &= 1 \ \text{cm} \\
\text{Number of small squares} &= 2 \times 2 \\
                              &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

For the equilateral triangles, the $3\ \text{cm}$ triangle and the $1\ \text{cm}$ triangles are similar shapes.

The scale factor for the side is

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Scale factor for side} &= \dfrac{3} {1} \\
\text{Number of small triangles} &= 3 \times 3 \\
                                 &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

So $9$ small $1\ \text{cm}$ equilateral triangles fit inside the $3\ \text{cm}$ equilateral triangle.
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$9$


</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

</div>

<div class="formulacodeblock_title">

</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workingcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="workingcodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Working Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">In the square:
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Side of big square} &= 2 \ \text{cm} \\
\text{Side of small square} &= 1 \ \text{cm} \\
\text{Number of small squares} &= 2 \times 2 \\
                              &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
For the equilateral triangles, the $3\ \text{cm}$ triangle and the $1\ \text{cm}$ triangles are similar shapes.
<emptyline>
The scale factor for the side is
<emptyline>

\text{Number of small triangles} &= 3 \times 3 \\
                                 &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
So $9$ small $1\ \text{cm}$ equilateral triangles fit inside the $3\ \text{cm}$ equilateral triangle.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class="question" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

What is the area of a square that has a perimeter $28\ \text{cm}$? Give the units with your answer.

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">What is the area of a square that has a perimeter $28\ \text{cm}$? Give the units with your answer.
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

Let the length of a side of the sqaure $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}       &= 28 \ \text{cm} \\\\
4 \times a              &= 28 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {28} {4} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 7} {4} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 7} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                       &= 7 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text {Area}            &= a \times a \\\\
                        &= 7 \times 7 \\\\
                        &= 49 \ \text{cm}^{2}
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

$49 \ \text{cm}^{2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class="workingscodeblock">
<div class="tipcodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

</code></pre>

</div>

<emptyline>

4 \times a              &= 28 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {28} {4} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 7} {4} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 7} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a                       &= 7 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text {Area}            &= a \times a \\\\
                        &= 7 \times 7 \\\\
                        &= 49 \ \text{cm}^{2}

$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class="question_envelope rag_not_found subquestion">
<div class="question subquestion" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

I have a square piece of paper.

The diagram shows information about this square labelled $A$.

![papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s01-i00.png](/assets/papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s01-i00.png "papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s01-i00.png")

I fold square A in half to make rectangle $B$.

![papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s01-i01.png](/assets/papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s01-i01.png "papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s01-i01.png")

Then I fold rectangle B in half to make square $C$.

![papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s01-i02.png](/assets/papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s01-i02.png "papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s01-i02.png")

Complete the table below to show the area and perimeter of each shape.

|             |      Area      |   Perimeter  |
|:-------------:|:--------------:|:------------:|
| Square A    | $\cdots \ \text{cm}^2$ | $\cdots \ \text{cm}$ |
| Rectangle B | $\cdots \ \text{cm}^2$ | $\cdots \ \text{cm}$ |
| Square C    | $\cdots \ \text{cm}^2$ | $\cdots \ \text{cm}$ |

</div>
<div class="questioncodeblock" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">
<div class="questioncodeblock_title">
<strong style="display: block; margin-bottom: 0.5rem;">Question Raw</strong>
</div>
<pre class="language-latex"><code class="language-latex">I have a square piece of paper.
<emptyline>
The diagram shows information about this square labelled $A$.
<emptyline>
%image%
<emptyline>
I fold square A in half to make rectangle $B$.
<emptyline>
%image%
<emptyline>
Then I fold rectangle B in half to make square $C$.
<emptyline>
%image%
<emptyline>
Complete the table below to show the area and perimeter of each shape.
<emptyline>
|             |      Area      |   Perimeter  |
|:-------------:|:--------------:|:------------:|
| Square A    | $\cdots \ \text{cm}^2$ | $\cdots \ \text{cm}$ |
| Rectangle B | $\cdots \ \text{cm}^2$ | $\cdots \ \text{cm}$ |
| Square C    | $\cdots \ \text{cm}^2$ | $\cdots \ \text{cm}$ |
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class="workings">
<div class="tips placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Tips**

<div class="tip">
%empty%
</div>
</div>
<div class="formulas" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Formulas**

<div class="formula">
- ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$

- ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$

- ABBR: $\text{Perimeter} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$

- ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$
</div>
</div>
<div class="working" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Working**

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area of A}        &= 8 \times 8 \\\\
                        &= 64 \ \text{cm}^2 \\\\
\text{Perimeter of A}   &= 4 \times 8 \\\\
                        &= 32 \ \text{cm}  \\\\
\text{Area of B}        &= 8 \times 4 \\\\
                        &= 32 \ \text{cm}^2 \\\\
\text{Perimeter of B}   &= 2 \times (4 + 8) \\\\
                        &= 12 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 24 \ \text{cm}  \\\\
\text{Area of c}        &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                        &= 16 \ \text{cm}^2 \\\\
\text{Perimeter of C}   &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                        &= 16 \ \text{cm}  \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</div>
</div>
<div class="answers">
<div class="answer placeholder" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Answer**

</div>
</div>

<div class="question subquestion" style="margin: 1rem 0; padding: 0.85rem 1rem; border: 1px solid #9ea7b3; border-radius: 6px;">

**Question**

I start again with square $A$.

![papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s02-i00.png](/assets/papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s02-i00.png "papers/sevenoaks-school/2010/sample-paper-1/s01-q17-s02-i00.png")

These semi-circles overlap to create four petals which are shaded on the diagram. The area of each semi-circle is $3.5$ square units. Work out the shaded area.

**Question**

Emilia suggests that the number of squares in a pattern can be worked out by following these instructions, in order:

- Start with the pattern number, $\text{n}$.
- Add $1$ to give a new number, $\text{m}$.
- Multiply $\text{m}$ by $\text{n}$.
- Divide by $2$

Use the instructions to calculate the number of squares in pattern $100$.

</div>

One isosceles triangle has sides of length $5\ \text{cm}$, $5\ \text{cm}$, $8\ \text{cm}$ ; another has sides of length $5\ \text{cm}$, $5\ \text{cm}$, $6\ \text{cm}$. Show that the two triangles have the same area.
